This code is untrusted, so each user needs their own secure sandbox. Additionally, you want users to be able to run multiple requests in sequence, potentially writing to local files or saving in-memory state.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To do this, you need to create a container on-demand for each user session, then route subsequent requests to that container. Hereâs how you can accomplish this:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;First, you write some &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/containers-demos/blob/main/ai/wrangler.jsonc#L6\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;basic Wrangler config&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, then you route requests to containers via your Worker:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;import { Container } from &amp;quot;cloudflare:workers&amp;quot;;\n\nexport default {\n  async fetch(request, env) {\n    const url = new URL(request.url);\n\n    if (url.pathname.startsWith(&amp;quot;/execute-code&amp;quot;)) {\n      const { sessionId, messages } = await request.json();\n      // pass in prompt to get the code from Llama 4\n      const codeToExecute = await env.AI.run(&amp;quot;@cf/meta/llama-4-scout-17b-16e-instruct&amp;quot;, { messages });\n\n// get a different container for each user session\n      const id = env.CODE_EXECUTOR.idFromName(sessionId);\n      const sandbox = env.CODE_EXECUTOR.get(id);\n\n// execute a request on the container\n      return sandbox.fetch(&amp;quot;/execute-code&amp;quot;, { method: &amp;quot;POST&amp;quot;, body: codeToExecute });\n    }\n\n    // ... rest of Worker ...\n  },\n};\n\n// define your container using the Container class from cloudflare:workers\nexport class CodeExecutor extends Container {\ndefaultPort = 8080;\nsleepAfter = &amp;quot;1m&amp;quot;;\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Then, deploy your code with a single command: &lt;code&gt;wrangler deploy&lt;/code&gt;. This builds your container image, pushes it to Cloudflareâs registry, readies containers to boot quickly across the globe, and deploys your Worker.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Rust\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Rust\&quot;&gt;$ wrangler deploy&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Thatâs it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;How does it work?&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Your Worker creates and starts up containers on-demand. Each time you call &lt;code&gt;env.CODE_EXECUTOR.get(id)&lt;/code&gt; with a unique ID, it sends requests to a unique container instance. The container will automatically boot on the first &lt;code&gt;fetch&lt;/code&gt;, then put itself to sleep after a configurable timeout, in this case 1 minute. You only pay for the time that the container is actively running.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;When you request a new container, we boot one in a Cloudflare location near the incoming request. This means that low-latency workloads are well-served no matter the region. Cloudflare takes care of all the pre-warming and caching so you donât have to think about it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This allows each user to run code in their own secure environment.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;stateless-and-global-ffmpeg-everywhere\&quot;&gt;Stateless and global: FFmpeg everywhere&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#stateless-and-global-ffmpeg-everywhere\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        &lt;p&gt;Stateless and autoscaling applications work equally well on Cloudflare Containers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Imagine you want to run a container that takes a video file and turns it into an animated GIF using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.ffmpeg.org/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;FFmpeg&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Unlike the previous example, any container can serve any request, but you still donât want to send bytes across an ocean and back unnecessarily. So, ideally the app can be deployed everywhere.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To do this, you declare a container in Wrangler config and turn on &lt;code&gt;autoscaling&lt;/code&gt;. This specific configuration ensures that one instance is always running and if CPU usage increases beyond 75% of capacity, additional instances are added:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&amp;quot;containers&amp;quot;: [\n  {\n    &amp;quot;class_name&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;GifMaker&amp;quot;,\n    &amp;quot;image&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;./Dockerfile&amp;quot;, // container source code can be alongside Worker code\n    &amp;quot;instance_type&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;basic&amp;quot;,\n    &amp;quot;autoscaling&amp;quot;: {\n      &amp;quot;minimum_instances&amp;quot;: 1,\n      &amp;quot;cpu_target&amp;quot;: 75,\n    }\n  }\n],\n// ...rest of wrangler.jsonc...&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;To route requests, you just call &lt;code&gt;env.GIF_MAKER.fetch&lt;/code&gt; and requests are automatically sent to the closest container:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;import { Container } from &amp;quot;cloudflare:workers&amp;quot;;\n\nexport class GifMaker extends Container {\n  defaultPort: 1337,\n}\n\nexport default {\n  async fetch(request, env) {\n    const url = new URL(request.url);\n\n    if (url.pathname === &amp;quot;/make-gif&amp;quot;) {\n      return env.GIF_MAKER.fetch(request)\n    }\n\n    // ... rest of Worker ...\n  },\n};&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;going-beyond-the-basics\&quot;&gt;Going beyond the basics&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#going-beyond-the-basics\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        &lt;p&gt;From the examples above, you can see that getting a basic container service running on Workers just takes a few lines of config and a little Workers code. Thereâs no need to worry about capacity, artifact registries, regions, or scaling.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For more advanced use, weâve designed Cloudflare Containers to run on top of Durable Objects and work in tandem with Workers. Letâs take a look at the underlying architecture and see some of the advanced use cases it enables.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;durable-objects-as-programmable-sidecars\&quot;&gt;Durable Objects as programmable sidecars&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#durable-objects-as-programmable-sidecars\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;Routing to containers is enabled using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; under the hood. In the examples above, the &lt;code&gt;Container&lt;/code&gt; class from &lt;code&gt;cloudflare:workers&lt;/code&gt; just wraps a container-enabled Durable Object and provides helper methods for common patterns. In the rest of this post, weâll look at examples using Durable Objects directly, as this should shed light on the platformâs underlying design.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/tEAz34lNlHaJtLQVp1qia/734fc01c90e2aca8e5d75c060f09be9e/1.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1720\&quot; height=\&quot;860\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each Durable Object acts as a programmable sidecar that can proxy requests to the container and manages its lifecycle. This allows you to control and extend your containers in ways that are hard on other platforms.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can manually start, stop, and execute commands on a specific container by calling RPC methods on its Durable Object, which now has a new object at &lt;code&gt;this.ctx.container&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;class MyContainer extends DurableObject {\n\t// these RPC methods are callable from a Worker\n        async customBoot(entrypoint, envVars) {\n\t            this.ctx.container.start({ entrypoint, env: envVars });\n        }\n\n       async stopContainer() {\n                   const SIGTERM = 15;\n\t           this.ctx.container.signal(SIGTERM);\n        }\n\n        async startBackupScript() {\n\t            await this.ctx.container.exec([&amp;quot;./backup&amp;quot;]);\n        }\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;You can also monitor your container and run hooks in response to Container status changes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For instance, say you have a CI job that runs builds in a Container. You want to post a message to a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/queues/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Queue&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; based on the exit status. You can easily program this behavior:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;class BuilderContainer extends DurableObject {\n  constructor(ctx, env) {\n    super(ctx, env)\n    async function onContainerExit() {\n      await this.env.QUEUE.send({ status: &amp;quot;success&amp;quot;, message: &amp;quot;Build Complete&amp;quot; });\n    }\n\n    async function onContainerError(err) {\n      await this.env.QUEUE.send({ status: &amp;quot;error&amp;quot;, message: err});\n    }\n\n    this.ctx.container.start();\n    this.ctx.container.monitor().then(onContainerExit).catch(onContainerError); \n  }\n\n  async isRunning() { return this.ctx.container.running; }\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;And lastly, if you have state that needs to be loaded into a container each time it runs, you can use status hooks to persist state from the container before it sleeps and to reload state into the container after it starts:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;import { startAndWaitForPort } from &amp;quot;./helpers&amp;quot;\n\nclass MyContainer extends DurableObject {\n  constructor(ctx, env) {\n    super(ctx, env)\n    this.ctx.blockConcurrencyWhile(async () =&amp;gt; {\n      this.ctx.storage.sql.exec(&amp;#039;CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS state (value TEXT)&amp;#039;);\n      this.ctx.storage.sql.exec(&amp;#039;INSERT INTO state (value) SELECT &amp;#039;&amp;#039; WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT * FROM state)&amp;#039;);\n      await startAndWaitForPort(this.ctx.container, 8080);\n      await this.setupContainer();\n      this.ctx.container.monitor().then(this.onContainerExit); \n    });\n  }\n\n  async setupContainer() {\n    const initialState = this.ctx.storage.sql.exec(&amp;#039;SELECT * FROM state LIMIT 1&amp;#039;).one().value;\n    return this.ctx.container\n      .getTcpPort(8080)\n      .fetch(&amp;quot;http://container/state&amp;quot;, { body: initialState, method: &amp;#039;POST&amp;#039; });\n  }\n\n  async onContainerExit() {\n    const response = await this.ctx.container\n      .getTcpPort(8080)\n      .fetch(&amp;#039;http://container/state&amp;#039;);\n    const newState = await response.text();\n    this.ctx.storage.sql.exec(&amp;#039;UPDATE state SET value = ?&amp;#039;, newState);\n  }\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;building-around-your-containers-with-workers\&quot;&gt;Building around your Containers with Workers&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#building-around-your-containers-with-workers\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;Not only do Durable Objects allow you to have fine-grained control over the Container lifecycle, the whole Workers platform allows you to extend routing and scheduling behavior as you see fit.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;using-workers-as-an-api-gateway\&quot;&gt;Using Workers as an API gateway&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#using-workers-as-an-api-gateway\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        &lt;p&gt;Workers provide programmable ingress logic from &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/network/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;over 300 locations&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; around the world. In this sense, they provide similar functionality to an API gateway.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For instance, letâs say you want to route requests to a different version of a container based on information in a header. This is accomplished in a few lines of code:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;export default {\n  async fetch(request, env) {\n    const isExperimental = request.headers.get(&amp;quot;x-version&amp;quot;) === &amp;quot;experimental&amp;quot;;\n    \n    if (isExperimental) {\n      return env.MY_SERVICE_EXPERIMENTAL.fetch(request);\n    } else {\n      return env.MY_SERVICE_STANDARD.fetch(request);\n    }\n  },\n};&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Or you want to rate limit and authenticate requests to the container:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;async fetch(request, env) {\n  const url = new URL(request.url);\n\n  if (url.pathname.startsWith(&amp;#039;/api/&amp;#039;)) {\n    const token = request.headers.get(&amp;quot;token&amp;quot;);\n\n    const isAuthenticated = await authenticateRequest(token);\n    if (!isAuthenticated) {\n      return new Response(&amp;quot;Not authenticated&amp;quot;, { status: 401 });\n    }\n\n    const { withinRateLimit } = await env.MY_RATE_LIMITER.limit({ key: token });\n    if (!withinRateLimit) {\n      return new Response(&amp;quot;Rate limit exceeded for token&amp;quot;, { status: 429 });\n    }\n\n    return env.MY_APP.fetch(request);\n  }\n  // ...\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;using-workers-as-a-service-mesh\&quot;&gt;Using Workers as a service mesh&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#using-workers-as-a-service-mesh\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        \n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/56gJk7HEDJs4dsNXDkBX1x/7c9d6be661d0cd6baea13caeedf478c0/2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1000\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;By default, Containers are private and can only be accessed via Workers, which can connect to one of many container ports. From within the container, you can expose a plain HTTP port, but requests will still be encrypted from the end user to the moment we send the data to the containerâs TCP port in the host. Due to the communication being relayed through the Cloudflare network, the container does not need to set up TLS certificates to have secure connections in its open ports.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can connect to the container through a WebSocket from the client too. See &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/containers-demos/tree/main/websockets\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;this repository&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for a full example of using Websockets.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Just as the Durable Object can act as proxy &lt;i&gt;to the container&lt;/i&gt;, it can act as a proxy &lt;i&gt;from the container &lt;/i&gt;as well. When setting up a container, you can toggle Internet access off and ensure that outgoing requests pass through Workers.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;// ... when starting the container...\nthis.ctx.container.start({ \n  workersAddress: &amp;#039;10.0.0.2:8080&amp;#039;,\n  enableInternet: false, // &amp;#039;enableInternet&amp;#039; is false by default\n});\n\n// ... container requests to &amp;#039;10.0.0.2:8080&amp;#039; securely route to a different service...\noverride async onContainerRequest(request: Request) {\n const containerId = this.env.SUB_SERVICE.idFromName(request.headers[&amp;#039;X-Account-Id&amp;#039;]);\n return this.env.SUB_SERVICE.get(containerId).fetch(request);\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;You can ensure all traffic in and out of your container is secured and encrypted end to end without having to deal with networking yourself.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/9PzlxveRZ6NNBiNeZTiWs/c70e5db9cc7d63c02a21f21769801bc4/3.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1000\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;This allows you to protect and connect containers within Cloudflareâs networkâ¦ or even when connecting to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-virtual-private-cloud\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;external private networks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;using-workers-as-an-orchestrator\&quot;&gt;Using Workers as an orchestrator&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#using-workers-as-an-orchestrator\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        &lt;p&gt;You might require custom scheduling and scaling logic that goes beyond what Cloudflare provides out of the box.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We donât want you having to manage complex chains of API calls or writing an &lt;a href=\&quot;https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/extend-kubernetes/operator/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;operator&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to get the logic you need. Just write some Worker code.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For instance, imagine your containers have a long startup period that involves loading data from an external source. You need to pre-warm containers manually, and need control over the specific region to prewarm. Additionally, you need to set up manual health checks that are accessible via Workers. Youâre able to achieve this fairly simply with Workers and Durable Objects.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;import { Container, DurableObject } from &amp;quot;cloudflare:workers&amp;quot;;\n\n// A singleton Durable Object to manage and scale containers\n\nclass ContainerManager extends DurableObject {\n  scale(region, instanceCount) {\n    for (let i = 0; i &amp;lt; instanceCount; i++) {\n      const containerId = env.CONTAINER.idFromName(`instance-${region}-${i}`);\n      // spawns a new container with a location hint\n      await env.CONTAINER.get(containerId, { locationHint: region }).start();\n    }\n  }\n\n  async setHealthy(containerId, isHealthy) {\n    await this.ctx.storage.put(containerId, isHealthy);\n  }\n}\n\n// A Container class for the underlying compute\n\nclass MyContainer extends Container {\n  defaultPort = 8080;\n\n  async onContainerStart() {\n    // run healthcheck every 500ms\n    await this.scheduleEvery(0.5, &amp;#039;healthcheck&amp;#039;);\n  }\n\n  async healthcheck() {\n    const manager = this.env.MANAGER.get(\n      this.env.MANAGER.idFromName(&amp;quot;manager&amp;quot;)\n    );\n    const id = this.ctx.id.toString();\n\n    await this.container.fetch(&amp;quot;/_health&amp;quot;)\n      .then(() =&amp;gt; manager.setHealthy(id, true))\n      .catch(() =&amp;gt; manager.setHealthy(id, false));\n  }\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;ContainerManager &lt;/code&gt;Durable Object exposes the &lt;code&gt;scale&lt;/code&gt; RPC call, which you can call as needed with a &lt;code&gt;region&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;instanceCount&lt;/code&gt; which scales up the number of active Container instances in a given region &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/reference/data-location/#provide-a-location-hint\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;using a location hint&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. The &lt;code&gt;this.schedule&lt;/code&gt; code executes a manually defined &lt;code&gt;healthcheck&lt;/code&gt; method on the Container and tracks its state in the Manager for use by other logic in your system.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These building blocks let users handle complex scheduling logic themselves. For a more detailed example using standard Durable Objects, see &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/containers-demos/tree/main/load-balancer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;this repository&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We are excited to see the patterns you come up with when orchestrating complex applications built with containers, and trust that between Workers and Durable Objects, youâll have the tools you need.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;integrating-with-more-of-cloudflares-developer-platform\&quot;&gt;Integrating with more of Cloudflareâs Developer Platform&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#integrating-with-more-of-cloudflares-developer-platform\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;Since it is &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/welcome-to-developer-week-2025/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Developer Week 2025&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, we would be remiss to not talk about &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workflows/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workflows&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, which &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/workflows-ga-production-ready-durable-execution/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;just went GA&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://agents.cloudflare.com/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, which &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/building-ai-agents-with-mcp-authn-authz-and-durable-objects/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;just got even better&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Letâs finish up by taking a quick look at how you can integrate Containers with these two tools.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;running-a-short-lived-job-with-workflows-r2\&quot;&gt;Running a short-lived job with Workflows &amp;amp; R2&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#running-a-short-lived-job-with-workflows-r2\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        &lt;p&gt;You need to download a large file from &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;R2&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, compress it, and upload it. You want to ensure that this succeeds, but donât want to write retry logic and error handling yourself. Additionally, you donât want to deal with rotating R2 API tokens or worry about network connections â it should be secure by default.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is a perfect opportunity for a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workflows/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workflow&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; using Containers. The container can do the heavy lifting of compressing files, Workers can stream the data to and from R2, and the Workflow can ensure durable execution.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;export class EncoderWorkflow extends WorkflowEntrypoint&amp;lt;Env, Params&amp;gt; {\n    async run(event: WorkflowEvent&amp;lt;Params&amp;gt;, step: WorkflowStep) {\n        const id = this.env.ENCODER.idFromName(event.instanceId);\n        const container = this.env.ENCODER.get(id);\n\n        await step.do(&amp;#039;init container&amp;#039;, async () =&amp;gt; {\n            await container.init();\n        });\n\n        await step.do(&amp;#039;compress the object with zstd&amp;#039;, async () =&amp;gt; {\n            await container.ensureHealthy();\n            const object = await this.env.ARTIFACTS.get(event.payload.r2Path);\n            const result = await container.fetch(&amp;#039;http://encoder/zstd&amp;#039;, {\n                method: &amp;#039;POST&amp;#039;, body: object.body \n            });\n            await this.env.ARTIFACTS.put(`results${event.payload.r2Path}`, result.body);\n        });\n\n        await step.do(&amp;#039;cleanup container&amp;#039;, async () =&amp;gt; {\n            await container.destroy();\n        });\n    }\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;calling-a-container-from-an-agent\&quot;&gt;Calling a Container from an Agent&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#calling-a-container-from-an-agent\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        &lt;p&gt;Lastly, imagine you have an AI agent that needs to spin up cloud infrastructure (you like to live dangerously). To do this, you want to use &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/hashicorp/terraform\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Terraform&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, but since itâs run from the command line, you canât run it on Workers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;By defining a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/concepts/tools/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;tool&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, you can enable your Agent to run the shell commands from a container:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;// Make tools that call to a container from an agent\n\nconst createExternalResources = tool({\n  description: &amp;quot;runs Terraform in a container to create resources&amp;quot;,\n  parameters: z.object({ sessionId: z.number(), config: z.string() }),\n  execute: async ({ sessionId, config }) =&amp;gt; {\n    return this.env.TERRAFORM_RUNNER.get(sessionId).applyConfig(config);\n  },\n});\n\n// Expose RPC Methods that call to the container\n\nclass TerraformRunner extends DurableObject {\n  async applyConfig(config) {\n    await this.ctx.container.getTcpPort(8080).fetch(APPLY_URL, {\n      method: &amp;#039;POST&amp;#039;,\n      body: JSON.stringify({ config }),\n    });\n  }\n\n  // ...rest of DO...\n}&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Containers are so much more powerful when combined with other tools. Workers make it easy to do so in a secure and simple way.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;pay-for-what-you-use-and-use-the-right-tool\&quot;&gt;Pay for what you use and use the right tool&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#pay-for-what-you-use-and-use-the-right-tool\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;The deep integration between Workers and Containers also makes it easy to pick the right tool for the job with regards to cost.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;With Cloudflare Containers, you only pay for what you use. Charges start when a request is sent to the container or it is manually started. Charges stop after the container goes to sleep, which can happen automatically after a configurable timeout. This makes it easy to scale to zero, and allows you to get high utilization even with highly-variable traffic.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Containers are billed for every 10ms that they are actively running at the following rates:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Memory: $0.0000025 per GB-second&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;CPU: $0.000020 per vCPU-second&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Disk $0.00000007 per GB-second\n&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;After 1 TB of free data transfer per month, egress from a Container will be priced per-region. We&amp;#39;ll be working out the details between now and the beta, and will be launching with clear, transparent pricing across all dimensions so you know where you stand.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Workers are lighter weight than containers and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-pricing-scale-to-zero\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;save you money by not charging when waiting on I/O&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. This means that if you can, running on a Worker helps you save on cost. Luckily, on Cloudflare it is easy to route requests to the right tool.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h3 id=\&quot;cost-comparison\&quot;&gt;Cost comparison&lt;/h3&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#cost-comparison\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n        &lt;p&gt;Comparing containers and functions services on paper is always going to be an apples to oranges exercise, and results can vary so much depending on use case. But to share a real example of our own, a year ago when Cloudflare acquired Baselime, Baselime was a heavy user of AWS Lambda. By moving to Cloudflare, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/80-percent-lower-cloud-cost-how-baselime-moved-from-aws-to-cloudflare/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;they lowered their cloud compute bill by 80%&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Below we wanted to share one representative example that compares costs for an application that uses both containers and serverless functions together. Itâd be easy for us to come up with a contrived example that uses containers sub-optimally on another platform, for the wrong types of workloads. We wonât do that here. We know that navigating cloud costs can be challenging, and that cost is a critical part of deciding what type of compute to use for which pieces of your application.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In the example below, weâll compare Cloudflare Containers + Workers against Google Cloud Run, a very well-regarded container platform that weâve been impressed by.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;h4&gt;Example application&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;p&gt;Imagine that you run an application that serves 50 million requests per month, and each request consumes an average 500 ms of wall-time. Requests to this application are not all the same though â half the requests require a container, and the other half can be served just using serverless functions.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Requests per month&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Wall-time (duration)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Compute required&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Google Cloud&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;25 million&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;500ms&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Container + serverless functions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Containers + Workers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Google Cloud Run + Google Cloud Run Functions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;25 million&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;500ms&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Serverless functions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Workers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Google Cloud Run Functions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;&lt;h4&gt;Container pricing&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;p&gt;On both Cloud Run and Cloudflare Containers, a container can serve multiple requests. On some platforms, such as AWS Lambda, each container instance is limited to a single request, pushing cost up significantly as request count grows. In this scenario, 50 requests can run simultaneously on a container with 4 GB memory and half of a vCPU. This means that to serve 25 million requests of 500ms each, we need 625,000 seconds worth of compute&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In this example, traffic is bursty and we want to avoid paying for idle-time, so weâll use Cloud Runâs request-based pricing.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;\n&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Price per vCPU second&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Price per GB-second of memory&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Price per 1m requests&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Monthly Price for Compute + Requests&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare Containers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0.000020&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0.0000025&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0.30&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$20.00&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Google Cloud Run&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0.000024&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0.0000025&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0.40&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$23.75&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;* Comparison does not include free tiers for either provider and uses a single Tier 1 GCP region&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Compute pricing for both platforms are comparable. But as we showed earlier in this post, Containers on Cloudflare run anywhere, on-demand, without configuring and managing regions. Each container has a programmable sidecar with its own database, backed by Durable Objects. Itâs the depth of integration with the rest of the platform that makes containers on Cloudflare uniquely programmable.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;h4&gt;Function pricing&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;p&gt;The other requests can be served with less compute, and code written in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/languages/javascript/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;JavaScript&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/languages/typescript/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;TypeScript&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/languages/python/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Python&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; or &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/languages/rust/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Rust&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, so weâll use Workers and Cloud Run Functions.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These 25 million requests also run for 500 ms each, and each request spends 480 ms waiting on I/O. This means that Workers will &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/workers-pricing-scale-to-zero/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;only charge for 20 ms of âCPU-timeâ&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, the time that the Worker actually spends using compute. This ratio of low CPU time to high wall time is extremely common when building AI apps that make inference requests, or even when just building REST APIs and other business logic. Most time is spent waiting on I/O. Based on our data, we typically see Workers use less than 5 ms of CPU time per request vs seconds of wall time (waiting on APIs or I/O).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Cloud Run Function will use an instance with 0.083 vCPU and 128 MB memory and charge on both CPU-s and GiB-s for the full 500 ms of wall-time.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;\n&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Total Price for âwall-timeâ&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Total Price for âCPU-timeâ&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Total Price for Compute + Requests&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare Workers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;N/A&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0.83&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$8.33&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Google Cloud Run Functions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$1.44&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;N/A&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$11.44&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;* Comparison does not include free tiers and uses a single Tier 1 GCP region.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This comparison assumes you have configured Google Cloud Run Functions with a max of 20 concurrent requests per instance. On Google Cloud Run Functions, the maximum number of concurrent requests an instance can handle varies based on the efficiency of your function, and your own tolerance for tail latency that can be introduced by traffic spikes.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Workers automatically scale horizontally, donât require you to configure concurrency settings (and hope to get it right), and can run in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/en-gb/network/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;over 300 locations&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;h4&gt;A holistic view of costs&lt;/h4&gt;&lt;p&gt;The most important cost metric is the total cost of developing and running an application. And the only way to get the best results is to use the right compute for the job. So the question boils down to friction and integration. How easily can you integrate the ideal building blocks together?&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;As more and more software makes use of generative AI, and makes inference requests to LLMs, modern applications must communicate and integrate with a myriad of services. Most systems are increasingly real-time and chatty, often holding open long-lived connections, performing tasks in parallel. Running an instance of an application in a VM or container and calling it a day might have worked 10 years ago, but when we talk to developers in 2025, they are most often bringing many forms of compute to the table for particular use cases.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This shows the importance of picking a platform where you can seamlessly shift traffic from one source of compute to another. If you want to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/rate-limit/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;rate-limit&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/full-stack-development-on-cloudflare-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;serve server-side rendered pages, API responses and static assets&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, handle authentication and authorization, make&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-ai/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt; inference requests to AI models&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, run core business logic via &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/workflows-ga-production-ready-durable-execution/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workflows&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, or &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-acquires-arroyo-pipelines-streaming-ingestion-beta/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;ingest streaming data&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, just handle the request in Workers. Save the heavier compute only for where it is actually the only option. With Cloudflare Workers and Containers, this is as simple as an if-else statement in your Worker. This makes it easy to pick the right tool for the job.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;coming-june-2025\&quot;&gt;Coming June 2025&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#coming-june-2025\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;We are collecting feedback and putting the finishing touches on our APIs now, and will release the open beta to the public in late June 2025.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;From day one of building Cloudflare Workers, itâs been our goal to build an integrated platform, where Cloudflare products work together as a system, rather than just as a collection of separate products. Weâve taken this same approach with Containers, and aim to make Cloudflare not only the best place to deploy containers across the globe, but the best place to deploy the types of complete applications that developers are building, that use containers in tandem with serverless functions, &lt;a href=\&quot;http://developers.cloudflare.com/workflows\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workflows&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://agents.cloudflare.com/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;much more&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâre excited to get this into your hands soon. Cloudflare plays a pivotal role for countless applications, empowering founders and engineering teams to build, scale, and accelerate their innovations with ease â and without the burden of technical overhead. And when applicable, initiatives like our &lt;a href=\&quot;http://www.cloudflare.com/forstartups\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Startup Program&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=\&quot;http://www.cloudflare.com/lp/workers-launchpad\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers Launchpad&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; offer the tooling and resources that further fuel these ambitious projects.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare recently announced&lt;b&gt; &lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/build-ai-agents-on-cloudflare/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, allowing developers to leverage Cloudflare to deploy agents to complete autonomous tasks. Weâre already seeing some great examples of startups leveraging Cloudflare as their platform of choice to invest in building their agent infrastructure. Read on to see how a few up-and-coming startups are building their AI agent platforms, powered by Cloudflare.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;lamatic-ai-built-a-scalable-ai-agent-platform-using-workers-for-platform\&quot;&gt;Lamatic AI built a scalable AI agent platform using Workers for Platform&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#lamatic-ai-built-a-scalable-ai-agent-platform-using-workers-for-platform\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;Founded in 2023, Lamatic.ai empowers SaaS startups to seamlessly integrate intelligent AI agents into their products. Lamatic.ai simplifies the deployment of AI agents by offering a fully managed lifecycle with scalability and security in mind. SaaS providers have been leveraging Lamatic to replatform their AI workflows via a no-code visual builder to reduce technical debt and ship products faster. Designed for high availability, scalability, and low latency, Lamaticâs architecture enables developers to build AI-driven applications that remain performant under heavy load. After acquiring a high amount of users in a short amount of time on Product Hunt, Lamatic identified there was real interest to solve complex problems with AI Agents, and the team knew they needed to build a solution with scalability and performance in mind.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare plays a key role in supporting Lamaticâs growth. Powered by Cloudflare &lt;a href=\&quot;https://workers.cloudflare.com/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;b&gt;, &lt;/b&gt;Lamatic ensures requests process closer to end users, minimizing latency while offloading computational strain from centralized servers. In just a few months, Lamatic.ai has efficiently scaled to over three million serverless requests per month, supporting over 1,000 customers â all managed by a lean three-person team.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Customers design their Agent Flows through a no-code visual builder, which generates an interoperable YAML configuration. Sensitive credentials such as API keys and model access tokens are securely encrypted and stored in Workers KV, ensuring they are only decrypted at runtime for enhanced security. All YAML configurations are then compiled into a Workers-compatible JavaScript bundle. When a project is deployed, Lamatic orchestrates critical components like sync jobs for scheduled data ETL operations and incoming webhooks to handle event-driven workflows via &lt;b&gt;Cloudflare &lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/queues/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Queues&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Once deployed, the project is fully operational as a Cloudflare Worker with an exposed API endpoint, allowing customers to integrate AI-powered automation directly into their applications with minimal friction.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/3YUQpvrLjYaee5RJq4TRW3/8d2db69af7607b038855a0f4f1e27dc6/1.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1738\&quot; height=\&quot;1043\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;To scale out their platform, Lamatic.ai built their architecture isolating serverless and AI logic on a per-customer basis. Rather than batching requests into a centralized cluster, Lamatic.ai distributes workloads across Cloudflareâs global network, ensuring each customer and endpoint is served by its own Worker executing dedicated logic. This per-customer deployment model â enabled by &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-for-platforms/workers-for-platforms/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers for Platforms&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;b&gt; &lt;/b&gt;â allows Lamatic.ai to deliver customer-specific serverless functions at scale, and reduces technical overhead as they onboard additional customers. Each customer gets a dedicated Worker whose request and rate limits are enabled based on their level of subscription.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Beyond request processing, Lamatic uses &lt;b&gt;Cloudflare &lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/kv/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers KV&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt; as a distributed config store to ensure high availability and security. All values are encrypted at rest with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galois/Counter_Mode\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AES-256-GCM&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and decrypted only at runtime, keeping operations both secure and low-latency. Tokens and user credentials are encrypted and stored in the database and KV.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To further enhance performance, Cloudflare &lt;b&gt;Queues&lt;/b&gt; plays a key role in orchestrating task completion. Lamatic uses Queues to offload work from Workers requests, and handle tasks such as webhooks and coordinating distributed processes, both essential for maintaining system consistency and reliability at scale. While Workers handle sync requests at point of execution, longer running jobs process via Queues. For example, during a scheduled ETL sync, new data records generated are stored as a message queue on Cloudflare &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/pub-sub/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Pub/Sub&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. A consumer Worker collects these messages and makes an API request to the pod using the Workers Queue. The consumer Worker consumes more messages as each queue is finished processing.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Another example of where this has been optimal is for managing AI workflows. Many AI workflows involve concurrent requests to multiple data sources, Queues streamlines data processing and efficiently feeds information into customersâ &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blogs.nvidia.com/blog/what-is-retrieval-augmented-generation/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; workflows. This approach smooths out workload spikes, reduces bottlenecks, and ensures that AI agents can reliably aggregate and process data without delays.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Beyond this, Lamatic.ai offers &lt;b&gt;Workers AI&lt;/b&gt; as one of the support inference providers that customers can use across their platform. Customers can choose to run one of the many open source models hosted on Workers AI, depending on their use case (chatbot, image generation, voice, etc.). Together, these layers solve the challenges of scaling AI agents by handling high volumes of data, maintaining low-latency responses, and ensuring robust security. With Cloudflareâs infrastructure as its backbone, Lamatic.ai has built a resilient and high-performing platform that meets the rigorous demands of modern AI applications, making it an ideal choice for startups embedding AI-driven features into their products.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;skyward-ai-automates-compliance-using-ai-agents-with-durable-objects-and-agents\&quot;&gt;Skyward AI automates compliance using AI agents with Durable Objects and &lt;code&gt;agents&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#skyward-ai-automates-compliance-using-ai-agents-with-durable-objects-and-agents\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;Skyward AI is transforming compliance operations by leveraging Cloudflareâs serverless computing capabilities to build AI-driven compliance agents that streamline critical tasks like evidence collection, real-time risk analysis, and policy updates. Compliance teams in fintech, supply chain, and other highly regulated industries use these AI Agents to extract and organize evidence, provide real-time recommendations, and orchestrate policy and procedural updates automatically. By handling document parsing, risk monitoring, and policy enforcement, these AI Agents reduce the risk of human error while allowing compliance professionals to focus on high-value tasks.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Skyward has built an AI agents platform designed with a serverless-first approach, avoiding the constraints of centralized cloud computing. To achieve this, the company leverages Cloudflareâs Developer Platform to create and maintain a highly responsive and scalable infrastructure. &lt;b&gt;Workers&lt;/b&gt; handle incoming requests like chat inputs, compliance checks, or authentication, and route them efficiently across multiple geographies. Skyward initially built their AI agents infrastructure using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;,&lt;b&gt; &lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workflows/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workflows&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and JavaScript-native RPC for AI coordination, but has recently transitioned to Cloudflareâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/build-ai-agents-on-cloudflare/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;new AI agents framework&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Given that &lt;code&gt;agents &lt;/code&gt;provides a framework for building and orchestrating AI agents, the migration has helped Skyward abstract the need to manage Durable Objects manually, significantly reducing time spent on managing these tools. While this release is fairly recent, the transition has helped simplify the way that agents communicate, but it also preserved the benefits of their original design like data privacy, isolation, and concurrency management. This has also made it easier to provide real-time feedback and responses to their end users.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Skyward optimizes real-time compliance automation by achieving sub-100 ms response times for AI agent queries. Workloads are structured to minimize unnecessary network round-trips, and a sync-engine approach proactively preloads and pushes data to clients, delivering a highly responsive user experience. To proxy AI inference, Skyward uses &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ai-gateway/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI Gateway&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to provide observability into usage, performance, and costs across multiple vendors, improving their AI operational efficiency. Leveraging Cloudflareâs serverless Developer Platform has allowed Skyward to simplify their architecture while supporting global availability, avoiding the need for Kubernetes clusters or complex locking mechanisms. The team also avoids the burden of managing regional deployments, as Cloudflareâs multi-region support ensures consistent performance worldwide without added operational complexity.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/IW8Nh4NECUH897s3OEETe/6e3823712b3fceca699c068605aa9092/2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1371\&quot; height=\&quot;551\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;State management is a critical component to execute agentic workflows. Each compliance session runs within a dedicated &lt;b&gt;Durable Object&lt;/b&gt;, which keeps relevant data close to the execution layer. This setup minimizes database round-trips and ensures that tasks like Anti-Money Laundering (AML) checks, Know Your Business (KYB) validation, and document processing remain efficient. Once a compliance session is complete, the system summarizes and stores the relevant information in Postgres and &lt;b&gt;R2&lt;/b&gt;, optimizing memory usage without requiring persistent cloud infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To balance low-latency operations with long-term storage, Skyward employs a multi-layered data management strategy. The Skyward team, using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/hyperdrive/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Hyperdrive&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, has been able to reduce query latency by nearly 50%, allowing compliance teams to receive immediate feedback. At the company&amp;#39;s core, Skywardâs goal is to offer a platform that is &amp;quot;streamlined for compliance teamsâ. The team maintains that a speedy feedback loop ensures end customers get the data and responses needed to act. Whether there&amp;#39;s one agent or hundreds of agents processing tasks in parallel, Hyperdrive ensures that database requests to assets like extensive company documentation (i.e. regulations, policies, procedures, internal documents), complex regulatory knowledge graphs, and on-demand context information for conversational workflows are all as performant as possible.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/b&gt; facilitate real-time session state, ensuring AI agents function smoothly without complex locking mechanisms. For larger compliance-related documents, such as legal PDFs and archived data, Cloudflare &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;R2&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt; provides long-term storage, ensuring only frequently accessed information remains readily available. This approach enhances performance while keeping storage management efficient and cost-effective.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Security and scalability remain priorities for compliance-focused AI applications. Skyward enforces strict access controls, ensuring that only authorized users can access development and production environments. Each AI session maintains an auditable log of key events, user actions, and approvals, supporting the ability to export these insights for compliance and legal requirements. Because each agent is deployed in its own instance and has its own database, Skyward ensures that there is a detailed record of every required user, agent interaction, and auditing requirements. On top of this, the ability to deploy and scale globally with Cloudflareâs network has allowed Skyward to maintain consistent, high-performance operations across multiple regions without extensive infrastructure overhead.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Looking ahead, Skyward plans to further enhance AI agent responsiveness by running select models directly on &lt;b&gt;Cloudflare Workers AI&lt;/b&gt;, reducing reliance on external inference providers. The team plans to further integrate &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-for-platforms/workers-for-platforms/\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers for Platforms&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in an effort to better isolate customer data and workflows, giving end users greater control over their compliance automation. As Cloudflare continues to evolve its AI capabilities, Skyward aims to push the boundaries of distributed AI compliance solutions, making regulatory adherence more automated, scalable, and secure.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n            &lt;h2 id=\&quot;building-on-cloudflare\&quot;&gt;Building on Cloudflare&lt;/h2&gt;\n            &lt;a href=\&quot;#building-on-cloudflare\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-left-5\&quot;&gt;\n              &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n            &lt;/a&gt;\n          &lt;/div&gt;\n          &lt;p&gt;Weâre inspired by how startups like Lamatic AI and Skyward AI are building their AI agent platforms on Cloudflare.
